AP Calculus AB - Unit 4 Canonical Content Bank

Derivatives in Context | Sections 4.1-4.5 | Canonical destination-first source

1. Notes Example / You Try It

Section 4.1 - Meaning of Derivatives

U4-S1-NOTE-EX01
Section 4.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

At $t=4$, $f(4)=12$ where $f(t)$ represents the height of a rising balloon. Interpret the value with units.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: At $t=4$ seconds, the height of a rising balloon is 12 meters.
Solution / scoring notes: A function value describes the original quantity, not a rate.
U4-S1-NOTE-YTI01
Section 4.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

At $t=3$, $f(3)=18$ where $f(t)$ represents the mass of salt in a solution. Interpret the value with units.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: At $t=3$ minutes, the mass of salt in a solution is 18 grams.
Solution / scoring notes: A function value describes the original quantity, not a rate.
U4-S1-NOTE-EX02
Section 4.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

For a model of concentration of a medication, $f'(5)=2$. Interpret this derivative in context and give units.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: At $t=5$ hours, the concentration of a medication is increasing at 2 milligrams per liter per hours.
Solution / scoring notes: The first derivative is the instantaneous rate of change of the modeled quantity.
U4-S1-NOTE-YTI02
Section 4.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

For a model of concentration of a medication, $f'(5)=-4$. Interpret this derivative in context and give units.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: At $t=5$ hours, the concentration of a medication is decreasing at 4 milligrams per liter per hours.
Solution / scoring notes: The first derivative is the instantaneous rate of change of the modeled quantity.
U4-S1-NOTE-EX03
Section 4.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

In a model of air temperature in a greenhouse, $f''(2)=-2$. Explain what this value measures and state its units.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: The rate of change of the air temperature in a greenhouse is decreasing at 2 degrees Celsius per minutes squared at $t=2$ minutes.
Solution / scoring notes: The second derivative is the rate of change of the first derivative.
U4-S1-NOTE-YTI03
Section 4.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

In a model of height of a rising balloon, $f''(4)=3$. Explain what this value measures and state its units.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: The rate of change of the height of a rising balloon is increasing at 3 meters per seconds squared at $t=4$ seconds.
Solution / scoring notes: The second derivative is the rate of change of the first derivative.
U4-S1-NOTE-EX04
Section 4.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

The model $f$ describes water depth in a tank. Use the table to distinguish the meanings of all three listed values.
QuantityValueUnits
$f(2)$$8$centimeters
$f'(2)$$2$centimeters per seconds
$f''(2)$$-3$centimeters per seconds squared

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: Function value = amount; first derivative = instantaneous rate of the amount; second derivative = instantaneous rate of change of that first rate.
Solution / scoring notes: Match derivative order to how many times the time-rate operation has been applied.
U4-S1-NOTE-YTI04
Section 4.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

The model $f$ describes volume of fuel in a reservoir. Use the table to distinguish the meanings of all three listed values.
QuantityValueUnits
$f(4)$$12$liters
$f'(4)$$3$liters per minutes
$f''(4)$$-3$liters per minutes squared

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: Function value = amount; first derivative = instantaneous rate of the amount; second derivative = instantaneous rate of change of that first rate.
Solution / scoring notes: Match derivative order to how many times the time-rate operation has been applied.

Section 4.2 - Motion

U4-S2-NOTE-EX01
Section 4.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A particle moves on a line with position $s(t)=2 t^{3} + 4 t^{2} + 3 t + 4$ meters. Find its velocity at $t=1$ seconds.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $17$ m/s
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate position: $v(t)=6 t^{2} + 8 t + 3$, then evaluate.
U4-S2-NOTE-YTI01
Section 4.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A particle moves on a line with position $s(t)=t^{3} + 3 t^{2} - 5 t - 3$ meters. Find its velocity at $t=2$ seconds.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $19$ m/s
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate position: $v(t)=3 t^{2} + 6 t - 5$, then evaluate.
U4-S2-NOTE-EX02
Section 4.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

For $s(t)=2 t^{3} - 2 t^{2} + t$ meters, find the acceleration at $t=1$ seconds.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $8$ m/s$^2$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate twice: $a(t)=4 \left(3 t - 1\right)$.
U4-S2-NOTE-YTI02
Section 4.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

For $s(t)=- t^{3} - 2 t^{2} - 5 t - 3$ meters, find the acceleration at $t=0$ seconds.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $-4$ m/s$^2$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate twice: $a(t)=- 2 \left(3 t + 2\right)$.
U4-S2-NOTE-EX03
Section 4.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

At one instant a particle has velocity $6$ m/s. State its speed and direction of motion.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $6$ m/s; positive direction
Solution / scoring notes: Speed is the magnitude of velocity; direction comes from the sign of velocity.
U4-S2-NOTE-YTI03
Section 4.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

At one instant a particle has velocity $-42$ m/s. State its speed and direction of motion.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $42$ m/s; negative direction
Solution / scoring notes: Speed is the magnitude of velocity; direction comes from the sign of velocity.
U4-S2-NOTE-EX04
Section 4.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

At an instant, $v=6$ m/s and $a=-5$ m/s$^2$. Is the particle speeding up or slowing down? Justify by signs.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: slowing down
Solution / scoring notes: Velocity and acceleration have opposite signs, so the particle is slowing down.
U4-S2-NOTE-YTI04
Section 4.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

At an instant, $v=-4$ m/s and $a=-1$ m/s$^2$. Is the particle speeding up or slowing down? Justify by signs.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: speeding up
Solution / scoring notes: Velocity and acceleration have the same signs, so the particle is speeding up.

Section 4.3 - Related Rates Intro

U4-S3-NOTE-EX01
Section 4.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

A circular oil slick has changing radius $r$ centimeters. Its radius increases at $3$ cm/min. At the instant $r=10$ cm, classify $r$, $dr/dt$, and $10$ as a quantity, a rate, or the instant-specific value.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $r$ is the changing quantity; $dr/dt=3$ cm/min is a rate; $r=10$ cm is the value at the instant.
Solution / scoring notes: Separate variable, derivative, and specific value before differentiating.
U4-S3-NOTE-YTI01
Section 4.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

A circular oil slick has changing radius $r$ centimeters. Its radius increases at $3$ cm/min. At the instant $r=10$ cm, classify $r$, $dr/dt$, and $10$ as a quantity, a rate, or the instant-specific value.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $r$ is the changing quantity; $dr/dt=3$ cm/min is a rate; $r=10$ cm is the value at the instant.
Solution / scoring notes: Separate variable, derivative, and specific value before differentiating.
U4-S3-NOTE-EX02
Section 4.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A square has side length $s$ that changes with time. Write a constraint equation relating area $A$ and $s$, then write its time-differentiated form. Do not substitute $s=8$ yet.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $A=s^2$, $dA/dt=2s\,ds/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: The changing variables remain symbolic through differentiation.
U4-S3-NOTE-YTI02
Section 4.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A square has side length $s$ that changes with time. Write a constraint equation relating area $A$ and $s$, then write its time-differentiated form. Do not substitute $s=10$ yet.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $A=s^2$, $dA/dt=2s\,ds/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: The changing variables remain symbolic through differentiation.
U4-S3-NOTE-EX03
Section 4.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A ladder of fixed length $13$ ft has horizontal distance $x$ and vertical height $y$. Write the geometric constraint and its derivative with respect to time.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $x^2+y^2=169$, $2x\,dx/dt+2y\,dy/dt=0$
Solution / scoring notes: The fixed ladder length is a constant, while x and y change with time.
U4-S3-NOTE-YTI03
Section 4.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A ladder of fixed length $13$ ft has horizontal distance $x$ and vertical height $y$. Write the geometric constraint and its derivative with respect to time.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $x^2+y^2=169$, $2x\,dx/dt+2y\,dy/dt=0$
Solution / scoring notes: The fixed ladder length is a constant, while x and y change with time.
U4-S3-NOTE-EX04
Section 4.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

For a sphere with changing radius $r$, identify which of $4\pi r^2$, $dr/dt$, and $dV/dt$ are a quantity/formula or rates. Then name the standard volume constraint that links the changing quantities.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $4\pi r^2$ is surface area, $dr/dt$ and $dV/dt$ are rates; volume constraint $V=\frac43\pi r^3$.
Solution / scoring notes: A rate contains a time derivative; a geometric quantity does not.
U4-S3-NOTE-YTI04
Section 4.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

For a sphere with changing radius $r$, identify which of $4\pi r^2$, $dr/dt$, and $dV/dt$ are a quantity/formula or rates. Then name the standard volume constraint that links the changing quantities.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Solution / scoring notes: A rate contains a time derivative; a geometric quantity does not.

Section 4.4 - Related Rates Problems

U4-S4-NOTE-EX01
Section 4.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

The radius of a circle increases at $3$ cm/s. Find the rate of change of area when $r=3$ cm.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $18 \pi$ cm$^2$/s
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate $A=\pi r^2$: $dA/dt=2\pi r\,dr/dt$, then substitute.
U4-S4-NOTE-YTI01
Section 4.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

The radius of a circle increases at $4$ cm/s. Find the rate of change of area when $r=3$ cm.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $24 \pi$ cm$^2$/s
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate $A=\pi r^2$: $dA/dt=2\pi r\,dr/dt$, then substitute.
U4-S4-NOTE-EX02
Section 4.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A spherical balloon has radius increasing at $3$ cm/s. How fast is its volume changing when $r=2$ cm?

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $48 \pi$ cm$^3$/s
Solution / scoring notes: From $V=\frac43\pi r^3$, $dV/dt=4\pi r^2\,dr/dt$.
U4-S4-NOTE-YTI02
Section 4.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A spherical balloon has radius increasing at $3$ cm/s. How fast is its volume changing when $r=2$ cm?

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $48 \pi$ cm$^3$/s
Solution / scoring notes: From $V=\frac43\pi r^3$, $dV/dt=4\pi r^2\,dr/dt$.
U4-S4-NOTE-EX03
Section 4.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A $13$-ft ladder rests against a wall. The bottom moves away at $2$ ft/s. When the bottom is $12$ ft from the wall, how fast is the top moving?

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $- \frac{24}{5}$ ft/s
Solution / scoring notes: Use $x^2+y^2=169$; differentiate to $x\,dx/dt+y\,dy/dt=0$. The negative result means downward.
U4-S4-NOTE-YTI03
Section 4.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A $13$-ft ladder rests against a wall. The bottom moves away at $1$ ft/s. When the bottom is $5$ ft from the wall, how fast is the top moving?

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $- \frac{5}{12}$ ft/s
Solution / scoring notes: Use $x^2+y^2=169$; differentiate to $x\,dx/dt+y\,dy/dt=0$. The negative result means downward.
U4-S4-NOTE-EX04
Section 4.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Water height in a cylinder of fixed radius $2$ m rises at $3$ m/min. Find $dV/dt$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $12 \pi$ m$^3$/min
Solution / scoring notes: Use $V=\pi r^2h$ with fixed radius and differentiate.
U4-S4-NOTE-YTI04
Section 4.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Water height in a cylinder of fixed radius $4$ m rises at $2$ m/min. Find $dV/dt$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $32 \pi$ m$^3$/min
Solution / scoring notes: Use $V=\pi r^2h$ with fixed radius and differentiate.

Section 4.5 - Linearization and L'Hospital's Rule

U4-S5-NOTE-EX01
Section 4.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Given $f(2)=10$ and $f'(2)=4$, use the linearization at $a=2$ to estimate $f(\frac{11}{5})$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{54}{5}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $L(x)=f(a)+f'(a)(x-a)$.
U4-S5-NOTE-YTI01
Section 4.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Given $f(2)=2$ and $f'(2)=2$, use the linearization at $a=2$ to estimate $f(\frac{11}{5})$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{12}{5}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $L(x)=f(a)+f'(a)(x-a)$.
U4-S5-NOTE-EX02
Section 4.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Use the tangent line to $f(x)=\sqrt{x}$ at $x=9$ to estimate $\sqrt{\frac{451}{50}}$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{901}{300}$
Solution / scoring notes: Build $L(x)=f(a)+f'(a)(x-a)$ at the convenient square $9$.
U4-S5-NOTE-YTI02
Section 4.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Use the tangent line to $f(x)=\sqrt{x}$ at $x=4$ to estimate $\sqrt{\frac{199}{50}}$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{399}{200}$
Solution / scoring notes: Build $L(x)=f(a)+f'(a)(x-a)$ at the convenient square $4$.
U4-S5-NOTE-EX03
Section 4.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

The graph shows $f$ and its tangent line $L$ near $x=-2$. Explain why $L(x)$ is expected to be most accurate near the tangency input and less reliable farther away.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: The tangent line matches both the function value and instantaneous slope at the center, so local agreement is strongest there; accumulated curvature makes farther estimates less reliable.
Solution / scoring notes: Local linearity is a near-center approximation, not a global identity.
U4-S5-NOTE-YTI03
Section 4.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

The graph shows $f$ and its tangent line $L$ near $x=1$. Explain why $L(x)$ is expected to be most accurate near the tangency input and less reliable farther away.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: The tangent line matches both the function value and instantaneous slope at the center, so local agreement is strongest there; accumulated curvature makes farther estimates less reliable.
Solution / scoring notes: Local linearity is a near-center approximation, not a global identity.
U4-S5-NOTE-EX04
Section 4.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

Classify the limit form $0/0$ for purposes of deciding whether a direct application of L'Hospital's Rule is justified.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: indeterminate
Solution / scoring notes: Direct L'Hospital quotient forms are $0/0$ and $\infty/\infty$, provided the other conditions are satisfied.
U4-S5-NOTE-YTI04
Section 4.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

Classify the limit form $0/0$ for purposes of deciding whether a direct application of L'Hospital's Rule is justified.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: indeterminate
Solution / scoring notes: Direct L'Hospital quotient forms are $0/0$ and $\infty/\infty$, provided the other conditions are satisfied.

Section 4.1 - Meaning of Derivatives

Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
At $t=2$, $f(2)=40$ where $f(t)$ represents the water depth in a tank. Interpret the value with units.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: At $t=2$ seconds, the water depth in a tank is 40 centimeters.
Solution / scoring notes: A function value describes the original quantity, not a rate.
U4-S1-PS1-C02
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
For a model of water depth in a tank, $f'(2)=-4$. Interpret this derivative in context and give units.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: At $t=2$ seconds, the water depth in a tank is decreasing at 4 centimeters per seconds.
Solution / scoring notes: The first derivative is the instantaneous rate of change of the modeled quantity.
U4-S1-PS1-C03
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
In a model of mass of salt in a solution, $f''(3)=-3$. Explain what this value measures and state its units.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: The rate of change of the mass of salt in a solution is decreasing at 3 grams per minutes squared at $t=3$ minutes.
Solution / scoring notes: The second derivative is the rate of change of the first derivative.
U4-S1-PS1-C04
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
The model $f$ describes height of a rising balloon. Use the table to distinguish the meanings of all three listed values.
QuantityValueUnits
$f(4)$$8$meters
$f'(4)$$-4$meters per seconds
$f''(4)$$-2$meters per seconds squared

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Function value = amount; first derivative = instantaneous rate of the amount; second derivative = instantaneous rate of change of that first rate.
Solution / scoring notes: Match derivative order to how many times the time-rate operation has been applied.
U4-S1-PS1-C05
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 3
A student says, “$f'(3)=2$ means the mass of salt in a solution equals 2 grams.” Identify the conceptual error and rewrite the statement correctly.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: The derivative is a rate, so its units are grams per minutes, not grams; it describes how the mass of salt in a solution is changing at that instant.
Solution / scoring notes: The student confused a derivative with the original quantity.
U4-S1-PS1-L01
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 3
Name the outermost derivative rule for $H(x)=\left(x^{2} + 1\right)^{3} e^{2 x}$, then compute $H'(x)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Product rule; $2 \left(x^{2} + 1\right)^{2} \left(x^{2} + 3 x + 1\right) e^{2 x}$
Solution / scoring notes: The expression is an outer product, with chain rule inside both factors as needed.
U4-S1-PS1-L02
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
A particle has position $s(t)=2 t^{3} - 4 t^{2} - 2 t + 7$. Find velocity and acceleration at $t=2$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $v(2)=6$, $a(2)=16$
Solution / scoring notes: Velocity is the first derivative; acceleration is the second derivative.
U4-S1-PS1-L03
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
Differentiate $f(x)=\left(2 x + 1\right)^{3}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $f'(x)=6 \left(2 x + 1\right)^{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the chain rule, including the inner derivative.
U4-S1-PS1-L04
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
Find $F'(x)$ for $F(x)=\left(x^{2} + 1\right) e^{3 x}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\left(3 x^{2} + 2 x + 3\right) e^{3 x}$
Solution / scoring notes: Coordinate product and chain rules.
U4-S1-PS1-L05
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
For $x^2+y^2=16$, find $dy/dx$ by implicit differentiation.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-x/y$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate with respect to x, include the dependent-variable factor, and solve.
U4-S1-PS1-M01
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Evaluate $\lim_{x\to 2}\frac{x^{3} - 8}{x - 2}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $12$
Solution / scoring notes: Factor and cancel the common factor; the remaining expression approaches $12$.
U4-S1-PS1-M02
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Use the table to find $(fg)'(3)$.
QuantityValue
$f(3)$$2$
$f'(3)$$5$
$g(3)$$2$
$g'(3)$$-3$

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $4$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the product rule using the four table values.
U4-S1-PS1-M03
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
State the units of a derivative when the original quantity is measured in liters and the independent variable is measured in minutes.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: liters per minute
Solution / scoring notes: A derivative carries output units divided by input units.
U4-S1-PS1-M04
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
Determine whether $f(x)=x^{2} - x - 3$ is continuous at $x=-1$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Yes.
Solution / scoring notes: Polynomials are continuous at every real input.
U4-S1-PS1-M05
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Differentiate $P(x)=\left(x^{2} + 3 x - 3\right) \cos{\left(x \right)}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $P'(x)=\left(2 x + 3\right) \cos{\left(x \right)} + \left(- x^{2} - 3 x + 3\right) \sin{\left(x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule.
U4-S1-PS1-M06
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Differentiate $Q(x)=\frac{- 4 x + e^{x}}{x^{2} - 4}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $Q'(x)=\frac{2 x \left(4 x - e^{x}\right) + \left(x^{2} - 4\right) \left(e^{x} - 4\right)}{\left(x^{2} - 4\right)^{2}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the quotient rule and simplify.
U4-S1-PS1-M07
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
For $f(x)=5 x^{2} + 4 x - 2$, find $f'(0)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $4$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate term by term and evaluate at $x=0$.
U4-S1-PS1-M08
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
Find the average rate of change of $g(x)=4 x + 3$ on $[-3,1]$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $4$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{g(b)-g(a)}{b-a}$.
U4-S1-PS1-M09
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Evaluate $\lim_{x\to 5}\frac{x^{2} - 25}{x - 5}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $10$
Solution / scoring notes: Factor and cancel the common factor; the remaining expression approaches $10$.
U4-S1-PS1-M10
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Use the table to find $(fg)'(3)$.
QuantityValue
$f(3)$$-7$
$f'(3)$$0$
$g(3)$$6$
$g'(3)$$3$

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-21$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the product rule using the four table values.

Section 4.3 - Related Rates Intro

U4-S3-PS1-C01
Section 4.3 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
A circular oil slick has changing radius $r$ centimeters. Its radius increases at $3$ cm/min. At the instant $r=10$ cm, classify $r$, $dr/dt$, and $10$ as a quantity, a rate, or the instant-specific value.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $r$ is the changing quantity; $dr/dt=3$ cm/min is a rate; $r=10$ cm is the value at the instant.
Solution / scoring notes: Separate variable, derivative, and specific value before differentiating.
U4-S3-PS1-C02
Section 4.3 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
A square has side length $s$ that changes with time. Write a constraint equation relating area $A$ and $s$, then write its time-differentiated form. Do not substitute $s=6$ yet.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $A=s^2$, $dA/dt=2s\,ds/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: The changing variables remain symbolic through differentiation.
U4-S3-PS1-C03
Section 4.3 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
A ladder of fixed length $13$ ft has horizontal distance $x$ and vertical height $y$. Write the geometric constraint and its derivative with respect to time.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $x^2+y^2=169$, $2x\,dx/dt+2y\,dy/dt=0$
Solution / scoring notes: The fixed ladder length is a constant, while x and y change with time.
U4-S3-PS1-C04
Section 4.3 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
For a sphere with changing radius $r$, identify which of $4\pi r^2$, $dr/dt$, and $dV/dt$ are a quantity/formula or rates. Then name the standard volume constraint that links the changing quantities.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $4\pi r^2$ is surface area, $dr/dt$ and $dV/dt$ are rates; volume constraint $V=\frac43\pi r^3$.
Solution / scoring notes: A rate contains a time derivative; a geometric quantity does not.
U4-S3-PS1-C05
Section 4.3 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Water fills a cylindrical tank of fixed radius $4$ m. Height $h$ and volume $V$ change. Write a constraint suitable for related rates and its time derivative; keep the fixed radius as a constant.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $V=16\pi h$, $dV/dt=16\pi\,dh/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: Only the height and volume are time-dependent in this model.
U4-S3-PS1-L01
Section 4.3 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
The graph shows velocity versus time. Identify one displayed interval on which the particle moves in the positive direction and explain the graphical evidence.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Any displayed interval where the velocity graph lies above the time axis, with a matching explanation.
Solution / scoring notes: Positive velocity means motion in the positive direction.
U4-S3-PS1-L02
Section 4.3 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
The graph shows position versus time. Explain how the slope of this graph connects to the sign of velocity at a chosen displayed time.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Positive position-graph slope corresponds to positive velocity; negative slope corresponds to negative velocity; zero slope corresponds to zero velocity.
Solution / scoring notes: Velocity is the derivative of position.
U4-S3-PS1-L03

A student argues that a positive acceleration always means a particle is speeding up. Give a counterexample using signs of velocity and acceleration, and state the correct criterion.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: For example, velocity negative and acceleration positive gives slowing down. A particle speeds up when velocity and acceleration have the same sign.
Solution / scoring notes: Acceleration sign alone is insufficient; compare it with velocity sign.

Section 4.4 - Related Rates Problems

U4-S4-PS1-C01
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
The radius of a circle increases at $3$ cm/s. Find the rate of change of area when $r=3$ cm.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $18 \pi$ cm$^2$/s
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate $A=\pi r^2$: $dA/dt=2\pi r\,dr/dt$, then substitute.
U4-S4-PS1-C02
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
A spherical balloon has radius increasing at $1$ cm/s. How fast is its volume changing when $r=2$ cm?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $16 \pi$ cm$^3$/s
Solution / scoring notes: From $V=\frac43\pi r^3$, $dV/dt=4\pi r^2\,dr/dt$.
U4-S4-PS1-C03
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
A $13$-ft ladder rests against a wall. The bottom moves away at $3$ ft/s. When the bottom is $12$ ft from the wall, how fast is the top moving?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $- \frac{36}{5}$ ft/s
Solution / scoring notes: Use $x^2+y^2=169$; differentiate to $x\,dx/dt+y\,dy/dt=0$. The negative result means downward.
U4-S4-PS1-C04

Water height in a cylinder of fixed radius $2$ m rises at $3$ m/min. Find $dV/dt$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $12 \pi$ m$^3$/min
Solution / scoring notes: Use $V=\pi r^2h$ with fixed radius and differentiate.
U4-S4-PS1-C05
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
A rectangle has $L=8$ cm and $W=3$ cm at an instant. If $dL/dt=1$ cm/s and $dW/dt=-1$ cm/s, find $dA/dt$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-5$ cm$^2$/s
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate $A=LW$ using the product rule and substitute the instant data.
U4-S4-PS1-L01
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
Use the symbol table to explain why a numerical value for $r$ should normally be substituted after differentiating a relation between $r$ and $V$.
SymbolMeaning
$r$radius
$dr/dt$radius rate
$V$volume
$dV/dt$volume rate

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Substituting first can turn a changing quantity into a constant and erase its derivative factor.
Solution / scoring notes: Keep changing quantities symbolic until the time-derivative equation is established.
U4-S4-PS1-L02

A rectangle has changing length $L$ and width $W$. Its area is $A=LW$. Write the time-differentiated relation needed for a related-rate setup.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $dA/dt=L\,dW/dt+W\,dL/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate the product of two changing quantities with respect to time.

Answer: Keep $r$ symbolic, differentiate $dA/dt=2\pi r\,dr/dt$, then substitute the instant values.
Solution / scoring notes: Early substitution can incorrectly make a changing quantity appear constant.

Section 4.5 - Linearization and L'Hospital's Rule

U4-S5-PS1-C01
Section 4.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Given $f(3)=2$ and $f'(3)=2$, use the linearization at $a=3$ to estimate $f(\frac{14}{5})$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{8}{5}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $L(x)=f(a)+f'(a)(x-a)$.
U4-S5-PS1-C02
Section 4.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Use the tangent line to $f(x)=\sqrt{x}$ at $x=4$ to estimate $\sqrt{\frac{201}{50}}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{401}{200}$
Solution / scoring notes: Build $L(x)=f(a)+f'(a)(x-a)$ at the convenient square $4$.
U4-S5-PS1-C03
Section 4.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
The graph shows $f$ and its tangent line $L$ near $x=1$. Explain why $L(x)$ is expected to be most accurate near the tangency input and less reliable farther away.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: The tangent line matches both the function value and instantaneous slope at the center, so local agreement is strongest there; accumulated curvature makes farther estimates less reliable.
Solution / scoring notes: Local linearity is a near-center approximation, not a global identity.
U4-S5-PS1-C04
Section 4.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
Classify the limit form $\infty/\infty$ for purposes of deciding whether a direct application of L'Hospital's Rule is justified.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: indeterminate
Solution / scoring notes: Direct L'Hospital quotient forms are $0/0$ and $\infty/\infty$, provided the other conditions are satisfied.
U4-S5-PS1-C05
Section 4.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Evaluate $\lim_{x\to0}\frac{e^{3x}-1}{x}$. State the indeterminate form before using L'Hospital's Rule.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $3$
Solution / scoring notes: Direct substitution gives $0/0$. Differentiate numerator and denominator to get $3e^{3x}$, then evaluate at 0.
U4-S5-PS1-L01
Section 4.5 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 3
A circle expands with $dr/dt\gt0$. A student reports $dA/dt=-12\pi$ square units per second at a positive radius. Explain why the sign is inconsistent with the model, even before recomputing.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Because $dA/dt=2\pi r\,dr/dt$ and both $r$ and $dr/dt$ are positive, area must be increasing.
Solution / scoring notes: Use the differentiated constraint to test sign reasonableness.
U4-S5-PS1-L02
Section 4.5 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 3
For a sphere whose radius changes with time, compare the usefulness of $V=\frac43\pi r^3$ and $r=(3V/(4\pi))^{1/3}$ as starting constraints for finding $dV/dt$ from $dr/dt$. Choose one and defend the choice.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: The standard volume form is usually more efficient because differentiating gives $dV/dt=4\pi r^2\,dr/dt$ directly.
Solution / scoring notes: Both equivalent equations are valid on the physical domain, but one isolates the requested rate more directly.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: A complete response must show a valid context-specific constraint, its time derivative, substitution only afterward, and a sign/units interpretation.
Solution / scoring notes: This item assesses the complete routine rather than a single formula.

Practice Set 2

Section 4.1 - Meaning of Derivatives

U4-S1-PS2-C01
Section 4.1 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
A student says, “$f'(2)=2$ means the air temperature in a greenhouse equals 2 degrees Celsius.” Identify the conceptual error and rewrite the statement correctly.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: The derivative is a rate, so its units are degrees Celsius per minutes, not degrees Celsius; it describes how the air temperature in a greenhouse is changing at that instant.
Solution / scoring notes: The student confused a derivative with the original quantity.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: liters per minutes; liters per minutes squared
Solution / scoring notes: Each differentiation divides the units by one additional unit of time.

$t$$f(t)$$f'(t)$

$2$$8$$-2$
$3$$12$$0$

Solution / scoring notes: Use the context rather than generic labels alone.

A student argues that a positive acceleration always means a particle is speeding up. Give a counterexample using signs of velocity and acceleration, and state the correct criterion.

Answer: For example, velocity negative and acceleration positive gives slowing down. A particle speeds up when velocity and acceleration have the same sign.
Solution / scoring notes: Acceleration sign alone is insufficient; compare it with velocity sign.

Section 4.3 - Related Rates Intro

U4-S3-PS2-C01
Section 4.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Water fills a cylindrical tank of fixed radius $4$ m. Height $h$ and volume $V$ change. Write a constraint suitable for related rates and its time derivative; keep the fixed radius as a constant.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $V=16\pi h$, $dV/dt=16\pi\,dh/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: Only the height and volume are time-dependent in this model.
U4-S3-PS2-C02
Section 4.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Use the symbol table to explain why a numerical value for $r$ should normally be substituted after differentiating a relation between $r$ and $V$.
SymbolMeaning
$r$radius
$dr/dt$radius rate
$V$volume
$dV/dt$volume rate

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Substituting first can turn a changing quantity into a constant and erase its derivative factor.
Solution / scoring notes: Keep changing quantities symbolic until the time-derivative equation is established.
U4-S3-PS2-C03
Section 4.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
A rectangle has changing length $L$ and width $W$. Its area is $A=LW$. Write the time-differentiated relation needed for a related-rate setup.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $dA/dt=L\,dW/dt+W\,dL/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate the product of two changing quantities with respect to time.
U4-S3-PS2-C04
Section 4.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
A student immediately replaces $r$ by $5$ in $A=\pi r^2$ before differentiating a growing-circle problem. Explain what information can be lost and give the correct sequence.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Keep $r$ symbolic, differentiate $dA/dt=2\pi r\,dr/dt$, then substitute the instant values.
Solution / scoring notes: Early substitution can incorrectly make a changing quantity appear constant.
U4-S3-PS2-C05
Section 4.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 1
A circular oil slick has changing radius $r$ centimeters. Its radius increases at $3$ cm/min. At the instant $r=10$ cm, classify $r$, $dr/dt$, and $10$ as a quantity, a rate, or the instant-specific value.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $r$ is the changing quantity; $dr/dt=3$ cm/min is a rate; $r=10$ cm is the value at the instant.
Solution / scoring notes: Separate variable, derivative, and specific value before differentiating.

U4-S4-PS2-C01

A rectangle has $L=6$ cm and $W=5$ cm at an instant. If $dL/dt=2$ cm/s and $dW/dt=1$ cm/s, find $dA/dt$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $16$ cm$^2$/s
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate $A=LW$ using the product rule and substitute the instant data.
U4-S4-PS2-C02
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
A circle expands with $dr/dt\gt0$. A student reports $dA/dt=-12\pi$ square units per second at a positive radius. Explain why the sign is inconsistent with the model, even before recomputing.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Because $dA/dt=2\pi r\,dr/dt$ and both $r$ and $dr/dt$ are positive, area must be increasing.
Solution / scoring notes: Use the differentiated constraint to test sign reasonableness.
U4-S4-PS2-C03
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
For a sphere whose radius changes with time, compare the usefulness of $V=\frac43\pi r^3$ and $r=(3V/(4\pi))^{1/3}$ as starting constraints for finding $dV/dt$ from $dr/dt$. Choose one and defend the choice.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: The standard volume form is usually more efficient because differentiating gives $dV/dt=4\pi r^2\,dr/dt$ directly.
Solution / scoring notes: Both equivalent equations are valid on the physical domain, but one isolates the requested rate more directly.
U4-S4-PS2-C04
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Solve a related-rate problem in words: identify the constraint, show the differentiated equation before substitution, and explain how the sign of the final rate determines direction.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: A complete response must show a valid context-specific constraint, its time derivative, substitution only afterward, and a sign/units interpretation.
Solution / scoring notes: This item assesses the complete routine rather than a single formula.
U4-S4-PS2-C05
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
The radius of a circle increases at $4$ cm/s. Find the rate of change of area when $r=3$ cm.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $24 \pi$ cm$^2$/s
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate $A=\pi r^2$: $dA/dt=2\pi r\,dr/dt$, then substitute.
U4-S4-PS2-L01
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 2
A square has side length $s$ that changes with time. Write a constraint equation relating area $A$ and $s$, then write its time-differentiated form. Do not substitute $s=10$ yet.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $A=s^2$, $dA/dt=2s\,ds/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: The changing variables remain symbolic through differentiation.
U4-S4-PS2-L02
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 2
A ladder of fixed length $13$ ft has horizontal distance $x$ and vertical height $y$. Write the geometric constraint and its derivative with respect to time.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $x^2+y^2=169$, $2x\,dx/dt+2y\,dy/dt=0$
Solution / scoring notes: The fixed ladder length is a constant, while x and y change with time.
U4-S4-PS2-L03
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 1
For a sphere with changing radius $r$, identify which of $4\pi r^2$, $dr/dt$, and $dV/dt$ are a quantity/formula or rates. Then name the standard volume constraint that links the changing quantities.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $4\pi r^2$ is surface area, $dr/dt$ and $dV/dt$ are rates; volume constraint $V=\frac43\pi r^3$.
Solution / scoring notes: A rate contains a time derivative; a geometric quantity does not.
U4-S4-PS2-L04
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 2
Water fills a cylindrical tank of fixed radius $4$ m. Height $h$ and volume $V$ change. Write a constraint suitable for related rates and its time derivative; keep the fixed radius as a constant.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Make the concluding notation unambiguous about the point being discussed. Focus the response on the related setup cylinder requested here.

Answer: $V=16\pi h$, $dV/dt=16\pi\,dh/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: Only the height and volume are time-dependent in this model.
U4-S4-PS2-L05
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 2
Use the symbol table to explain why a numerical value for $r$ should normally be substituted after differentiating a relation between $r$ and $V$.
SymbolMeaning
$r$radius
$dr/dt$radius rate
$V$volume
$dV/dt$volume rate

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Write the final expression without an unnecessary decimal approximation. Focus the response on the related substitution timing requested here.

Answer: Substituting first can turn a changing quantity into a constant and erase its derivative factor.
Solution / scoring notes: Keep changing quantities symbolic until the time-derivative equation is established.
U4-S4-PS2-M01
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Mastery | DOK 2
Differentiate $P(x)=\left(x + 4\right) \left(x^{2} + 2\right)$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $P'(x)=3 x^{2} + 8 x + 2$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule.
U4-S4-PS2-M02
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Mastery | DOK 2
Differentiate $Q(x)=\frac{4 x + e^{x}}{x^{2} - 4}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $Q'(x)=\frac{- 2 x \left(4 x + e^{x}\right) + \left(x^{2} - 4\right) \left(e^{x} + 4\right)}{\left(x^{2} - 4\right)^{2}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the quotient rule and simplify.
U4-S4-PS2-M03
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Mastery | DOK 2
For $f(x)=x^{2} - 2 x + 4$, find $f'(0)$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $-2$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate term by term and evaluate at $x=0$.

Find the average rate of change of $g(x)=4 x - 5$ on $[-3,-1]$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $4$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{g(b)-g(a)}{b-a}$.

Evaluate $\lim_{x\to -5}\frac{x^{2} - 25}{x + 5}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $-10$
Solution / scoring notes: Factor and cancel the common factor; the remaining expression approaches $-10$.

U4-S4-PS2-M07

State the units of a derivative when the original quantity is measured in liters and the independent variable is measured in minutes.

U4-S4-PS2-M08
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Mastery | DOK 1
Determine whether $f(x)=x^{2} + 3 x + 4$ is continuous at $x=3$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Yes.

U4-S4-PS2-M09
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Mastery | DOK 2
Differentiate $P(x)=\left(4 x^{2} - 2\right) \sin{\left(x \right)}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $P'(x)=8 x \sin{\left(x \right)} + 2 \left(2 x^{2} - 1\right) \cos{\left(x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule.
U4-S4-PS2-M10
Section 4.4 | Practice Set 2 | Mastery | DOK 2
Differentiate $Q(x)=\frac{- 3 x + e^{x}}{x^{2} + 1}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $Q'(x)=\frac{2 x \left(3 x - e^{x}\right) + \left(x^{2} + 1\right) \left(e^{x} - 3\right)}{\left(x^{2} + 1\right)^{2}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the quotient rule and simplify.

Section 4.5 - Linearization and L'Hospital's Rule

U4-S5-PS2-C01
Section 4.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Evaluate $\lim_{x\to0}\frac{e^{2x}-1}{x}$. State the indeterminate form before using L'Hospital's Rule.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $2$
Solution / scoring notes: Direct substitution gives $0/0$. Differentiate numerator and denominator to get $2e^{2x}$, then evaluate at 0.
U4-S5-PS2-C02
Section 4.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Consider $\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{1x^2+1}{x^2-3}$. Is L'Hospital's Rule applicable directly, and if so, evaluate the limit.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Yes; $1$
Solution / scoring notes: The form is $\infty/\infty$. Differentiating numerator and denominator gives a ratio tending to $1$.
U4-S5-PS2-C03
Section 4.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
A student sees a quotient in $\lim_{x\to2}\frac{x^2+1}{x+3}$ and immediately applies L'Hospital's Rule. Critique the decision.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Direct substitution gives $5/5=1$, not an indeterminate form, so L'Hospital's Rule is not justified or needed.
Solution / scoring notes: Check the limit form before differentiating numerator and denominator.
U4-S5-PS2-C04
Section 4.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
Compare linearization and L'Hospital's Rule as methods: state what question each method answers and one condition or limitation that must be checked before use.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Linearization estimates a function locally from a tangent model; L'Hospital evaluates certain indeterminate quotient limits. Locality matters for linearization, and the quotient must have an appropriate indeterminate form plus rule conditions for L'Hospital.
Solution / scoring notes: The methods serve different purposes and are not interchangeable.
U4-S5-PS2-C05
Section 4.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Given $f(4)=10$ and $f'(4)=-2$, use the linearization at $a=4$ to estimate $f(\frac{41}{10})$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $\frac{49}{5}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $L(x)=f(a)+f'(a)(x-a)$.
U4-S5-PS2-L01
Section 4.5 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 2
A spherical balloon has radius increasing at $1$ cm/s. How fast is its volume changing when $r=4$ cm?

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $64 \pi$ cm$^3$/s
Solution / scoring notes: From $V=\frac43\pi r^3$, $dV/dt=4\pi r^2\,dr/dt$.
U4-S5-PS2-L02

A $13$-ft ladder rests against a wall. The bottom moves away at $2$ ft/s. When the bottom is $12$ ft from the wall, how fast is the top moving?

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $- \frac{24}{5}$ ft/s
Solution / scoring notes: Use $x^2+y^2=169$; differentiate to $x\,dx/dt+y\,dy/dt=0$. The negative result means downward.

Extra Practice

Section 4.1 - Meaning of Derivatives

U4-S1-XP-C01
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
A student says, “$f'(3)=5$ means the mass of salt in a solution equals 5 grams.” Identify the conceptual error and rewrite the statement correctly.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: The derivative is a rate, so its units are grams per minutes, not grams; it describes how the mass of salt in a solution is changing at that instant.
Solution / scoring notes: The student confused a derivative with the original quantity.
U4-S1-XP-C02
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1
If $g(t)$ measures number of visitors in a museum in people and $t$ is in hours, state the units of $g'(t)$ and $g''(t)$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: people per hours; people per hours squared
Solution / scoring notes: Each differentiation divides the units by one additional unit of time.
U4-S1-XP-C03
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
The table describes water depth in a tank. At $t=2$, what does the sign of $f'(2)$ say about the quantity at that instant?
$t$$f(t)$$f'(t)$
$1$$37$$-7$
$2$$40$$-6$
$3$$44$$-4$

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: It is decreasing at that instant.
Solution / scoring notes: The sign of the first derivative indicates the instantaneous direction of change.
U4-S1-XP-C04
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Write one sentence that clearly distinguishes $f(t)$, $f'(t)$, and $f''(t)$ when $f$ models volume of fuel in a reservoir.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: The three quantities are the amount, its instantaneous rate of change, and the instantaneous rate of change of that rate, respectively.
Solution / scoring notes: Use the context rather than generic labels alone.
U4-S1-XP-C05
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1
At $t=3$, $f(3)=18$ where $f(t)$ represents the number of visitors in a museum. Interpret the value with units.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: At $t=3$ hours, the number of visitors in a museum is 18 people.
Solution / scoring notes: A function value describes the original quantity, not a rate.
U4-S1-XP-L01
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 2
For $x^2+y^2=16$, find $dy/dx$ by implicit differentiation.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $-x/y$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate with respect to x, include the dependent-variable factor, and solve.
U4-S1-XP-L02
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 2
If $f(-1)=4$ and $f'(-1)=2$, find $(f^{-1})'(4)$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\frac{1}{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the reciprocal derivative at the corresponding original input.
U4-S1-XP-L03
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 2
Find $f''(x)$ for $f(x)=x^{4} + \sin{\left(2 x \right)}$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $12 x^{2} - 4 \sin{\left(2 x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate twice and preserve higher-order notation.
U4-S1-XP-L04
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 3
Name the outermost derivative rule for $H(x)=\left(x^{2} + 1\right)^{3} e^{2 x}$, then compute $H'(x)$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Product rule; $2 \left(x^{2} + 1\right)^{2} \left(x^{2} + 3 x + 1\right) e^{2 x}$
Solution / scoring notes: The expression is an outer product, with chain rule inside both factors as needed.
U4-S1-XP-L05
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 2
A particle has position $s(t)=- t^{3} - 2 t^{2} - 2 t + 7$. Find velocity and acceleration at $t=2$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $v(2)=-22$, $a(2)=-16$
Solution / scoring notes: Velocity is the first derivative; acceleration is the second derivative.
U4-S1-XP-M01
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Mastery | DOK 2
Differentiate $P(x)=- 2 \left(2 x + 1\right) \left(2 x^{2} - 3\right)$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $P'(x)=- 24 x^{2} - 8 x + 12$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule.
U4-S1-XP-M02
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Mastery | DOK 2
Differentiate $Q(x)=\frac{\sin{\left(x \right)} - 2}{x - 2}$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $Q'(x)=\frac{\left(x - 2\right) \cos{\left(x \right)} - \sin{\left(x \right)} + 2}{\left(x - 2\right)^{2}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the quotient rule and simplify.
U4-S1-XP-M03
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Mastery | DOK 2
For $f(x)=4 x^{2} - 3 x - 5$, find $f'(2)$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $13$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate term by term and evaluate at $x=2$.
U4-S1-XP-M04
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Mastery | DOK 1
Find the average rate of change of $g(x)=2 x - 2$ on $[-2,2]$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $2$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{g(b)-g(a)}{b-a}$.
U4-S1-XP-M05
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Mastery | DOK 2
Evaluate $\lim_{x\to 3}\frac{x^{4} - 81}{x - 3}$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $108$
Solution / scoring notes: Factor and cancel the common factor; the remaining expression approaches $108$.
U4-S1-XP-M06
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Mastery | DOK 2
Use the table to find $(fg)'(2)$.
QuantityValue
$f(2)$$0$
$f'(2)$$2$
$g(2)$$-5$
$g'(2)$$0$

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $-10$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the product rule using the four table values.
U4-S1-XP-M07
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Mastery | DOK 1
State the units of a derivative when the original quantity is measured in liters and the independent variable is measured in minutes.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: liters per minute
Solution / scoring notes: A derivative carries output units divided by input units.
U4-S1-XP-M08
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Mastery | DOK 1
Determine whether $f(x)=x^{2} + 3 x - 3$ is continuous at $x=3$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Yes.
Solution / scoring notes: Polynomials are continuous at every real input.
U4-S1-XP-M09
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Mastery | DOK 2
Differentiate $P(x)=\left(x^{2} + 3 x - 2\right) \cos{\left(x \right)}$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $P'(x)=\left(2 x + 3\right) \cos{\left(x \right)} + \left(- x^{2} - 3 x + 2\right) \sin{\left(x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule.
U4-S1-XP-M10
Section 4.1 | Extra Practice | Mastery | DOK 2
Differentiate $Q(x)=\frac{2 x^{2} + x - 4}{x + 4}$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $Q'(x)=\frac{2 \left(x^{2} + 8 x + 4\right)}{x^{2} + 8 x + 16}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the quotient rule and simplify.

U4-S2-XP-C02
Section 4.2 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
The graph shows velocity versus time. Identify one displayed interval on which the particle moves in the positive direction and explain the graphical evidence.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Any displayed interval where the velocity graph lies above the time axis, with a matching explanation.
Solution / scoring notes: Positive velocity means motion in the positive direction.
U4-S2-XP-C03
Section 4.2 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
The graph shows position versus time. Explain how the slope of this graph connects to the sign of velocity at a chosen displayed time.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Positive position-graph slope corresponds to positive velocity; negative slope corresponds to negative velocity; zero slope corresponds to zero velocity.
Solution / scoring notes: Velocity is the derivative of position.
U4-S2-XP-C04
Section 4.2 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
A student argues that a positive acceleration always means a particle is speeding up. Give a counterexample using signs of velocity and acceleration, and state the correct criterion.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: For example, velocity negative and acceleration positive gives slowing down. A particle speeds up when velocity and acceleration have the same sign.
Solution / scoring notes: Acceleration sign alone is insufficient; compare it with velocity sign.
U4-S2-XP-C05
Section 4.2 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
A particle moves on a line with position $s(t)=- t^{3} - 2 t^{2} + 5 t - 6$ meters. Find its velocity at $t=2$ seconds.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $-15$ m/s
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate position: $v(t)=- 3 t^{2} - 4 t + 5$, then evaluate.
U4-S2-XP-L01
Section 4.2 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 1
For a model of volume of fuel in a reservoir, $f'(4)=3$. Interpret this derivative in context and give units.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: At $t=4$ minutes, the volume of fuel in a reservoir is increasing at 3 liters per minutes.
Solution / scoring notes: The first derivative is the instantaneous rate of change of the modeled quantity.
U4-S2-XP-L02
Section 4.2 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 2
In a model of air temperature in a greenhouse, $f''(2)=2$. Explain what this value measures and state its units.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: The rate of change of the air temperature in a greenhouse is increasing at 2 degrees Celsius per minutes squared at $t=2$ minutes.
Solution / scoring notes: The second derivative is the rate of change of the first derivative.
U4-S2-XP-L03
Section 4.2 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 2
The model $f$ describes water depth in a tank. Use the table to distinguish the meanings of all three listed values.
QuantityValueUnits
$f(2)$$8$centimeters
$f'(2)$$-4$centimeters per seconds
$f''(2)$$2$centimeters per seconds squared

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Function value = amount; first derivative = instantaneous rate of the amount; second derivative = instantaneous rate of change of that first rate.
Solution / scoring notes: Match derivative order to how many times the time-rate operation has been applied.
U4-S2-XP-L04
Section 4.2 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 3
A student says, “$f'(6)=5$ means the electrical charge stored in a device equals 5 coulombs.” Identify the conceptual error and rewrite the statement correctly.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: The derivative is a rate, so its units are coulombs per seconds, not coulombs; it describes how the electrical charge stored in a device is changing at that instant.
Solution / scoring notes: The student confused a derivative with the original quantity.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: grams per minutes; grams per minutes squared
Solution / scoring notes: Each differentiation divides the units by one additional unit of time.

Section 4.3 - Related Rates Intro

U4-S3-XP-C01
Section 4.3 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Water fills a cylindrical tank of fixed radius $4$ m. Height $h$ and volume $V$ change. Write a constraint suitable for related rates and its time derivative; keep the fixed radius as a constant.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $V=16\pi h$, $dV/dt=16\pi\,dh/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: Only the height and volume are time-dependent in this model.
U4-S3-XP-C02
Section 4.3 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Use the symbol table to explain why a numerical value for $r$ should normally be substituted after differentiating a relation between $r$ and $V$.
SymbolMeaning
$r$radius
$dr/dt$radius rate
$V$volume
$dV/dt$volume rate

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Substituting first can turn a changing quantity into a constant and erase its derivative factor.
Solution / scoring notes: Keep changing quantities symbolic until the time-derivative equation is established.
U4-S3-XP-C03
Section 4.3 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
A rectangle has changing length $L$ and width $W$. Its area is $A=LW$. Write the time-differentiated relation needed for a related-rate setup.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $dA/dt=L\,dW/dt+W\,dL/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate the product of two changing quantities with respect to time.
U4-S3-XP-C04
Section 4.3 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
A student immediately replaces $r$ by $5$ in $A=\pi r^2$ before differentiating a growing-circle problem. Explain what information can be lost and give the correct sequence.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Keep $r$ symbolic, differentiate $dA/dt=2\pi r\,dr/dt$, then substitute the instant values.
Solution / scoring notes: Early substitution can incorrectly make a changing quantity appear constant.
U4-S3-XP-C05
Section 4.3 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $r$ is the changing quantity; $dr/dt=3$ cm/min is a rate; $r=10$ cm is the value at the instant.
Solution / scoring notes: Separate variable, derivative, and specific value before differentiating.

Section 4.4 - Related Rates Problems

U4-S4-XP-C01
Section 4.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
A rectangle has $L=10$ cm and $W=3$ cm at an instant. If $dL/dt=2$ cm/s and $dW/dt=1$ cm/s, find $dA/dt$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $16$ cm$^2$/s
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate $A=LW$ using the product rule and substitute the instant data.
U4-S4-XP-C02
Section 4.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
A circle expands with $dr/dt\gt0$. A student reports $dA/dt=-12\pi$ square units per second at a positive radius. Explain why the sign is inconsistent with the model, even before recomputing.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Because $dA/dt=2\pi r\,dr/dt$ and both $r$ and $dr/dt$ are positive, area must be increasing.
Solution / scoring notes: Use the differentiated constraint to test sign reasonableness.
U4-S4-XP-C03
Section 4.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
For a sphere whose radius changes with time, compare the usefulness of $V=\frac43\pi r^3$ and $r=(3V/(4\pi))^{1/3}$ as starting constraints for finding $dV/dt$ from $dr/dt$. Choose one and defend the choice.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: The standard volume form is usually more efficient because differentiating gives $dV/dt=4\pi r^2\,dr/dt$ directly.
Solution / scoring notes: Both equivalent equations are valid on the physical domain, but one isolates the requested rate more directly.
U4-S4-XP-C04
Section 4.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Solve a related-rate problem in words: identify the constraint, show the differentiated equation before substitution, and explain how the sign of the final rate determines direction.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: A complete response must show a valid context-specific constraint, its time derivative, substitution only afterward, and a sign/units interpretation.
Solution / scoring notes: This item assesses the complete routine rather than a single formula.
U4-S4-XP-C05
Section 4.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
The radius of a circle increases at $3$ cm/s. Find the rate of change of area when $r=8$ cm.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $48 \pi$ cm$^2$/s
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate $A=\pi r^2$: $dA/dt=2\pi r\,dr/dt$, then substitute.
U4-S4-XP-L01
Section 4.4 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 2
A square has side length $s$ that changes with time. Write a constraint equation relating area $A$ and $s$, then write its time-differentiated form. Do not substitute $s=12$ yet.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $A=s^2$, $dA/dt=2s\,ds/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: The changing variables remain symbolic through differentiation.
U4-S4-XP-L02
Section 4.4 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 2
A ladder of fixed length $13$ ft has horizontal distance $x$ and vertical height $y$. Write the geometric constraint and its derivative with respect to time.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $x^2+y^2=169$, $2x\,dx/dt+2y\,dy/dt=0$
Solution / scoring notes: The fixed ladder length is a constant, while x and y change with time.
U4-S4-XP-L03

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $4\pi r^2$ is surface area, $dr/dt$ and $dV/dt$ are rates; volume constraint $V=\frac43\pi r^3$.

Section 4.5 - Linearization and L'Hospital's Rule

U4-S5-XP-C01
Section 4.5 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Evaluate $\lim_{x\to0}\frac{e^{4x}-1}{x}$. State the indeterminate form before using L'Hospital's Rule.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $4$
Solution / scoring notes: Direct substitution gives $0/0$. Differentiate numerator and denominator to get $4e^{4x}$, then evaluate at 0.
U4-S5-XP-C02
Section 4.5 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Consider $\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{1x^2+1}{x^2-3}$. Is L'Hospital's Rule applicable directly, and if so, evaluate the limit.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Yes; $1$
Solution / scoring notes: The form is $\infty/\infty$. Differentiating numerator and denominator gives a ratio tending to $1$.
U4-S5-XP-C03
Section 4.5 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
A student sees a quotient in $\lim_{x\to2}\frac{x^2+1}{x+3}$ and immediately applies L'Hospital's Rule. Critique the decision.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Direct substitution gives $5/5=1$, not an indeterminate form, so L'Hospital's Rule is not justified or needed.
Solution / scoring notes: Check the limit form before differentiating numerator and denominator.
U4-S5-XP-C04
Section 4.5 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
Compare linearization and L'Hospital's Rule as methods: state what question each method answers and one condition or limitation that must be checked before use.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Linearization estimates a function locally from a tangent model; L'Hospital evaluates certain indeterminate quotient limits. Locality matters for linearization, and the quotient must have an appropriate indeterminate form plus rule conditions for L'Hospital.
Solution / scoring notes: The methods serve different purposes and are not interchangeable.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\frac{21}{5}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $L(x)=f(a)+f'(a)(x-a)$.

Water fills a cylindrical tank of fixed radius $4$ m. Height $h$ and volume $V$ change. Write a constraint suitable for related rates and its time derivative; keep the fixed radius as a constant.

Write the first mathematical step before carrying it out.

Answer: $V=16\pi h$, $dV/dt=16\pi\,dh/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: Only the height and volume are time-dependent in this model.
U4-S3-WU2-L01
Section 4.3 | Warm Up 2 | Last | DOK 2
The graph shows position versus time. Explain how the slope of this graph connects to the sign of velocity at a chosen displayed time.

Write the first mathematical step before carrying it out.

Answer: Positive position-graph slope corresponds to positive velocity; negative slope corresponds to negative velocity; zero slope corresponds to zero velocity.
Solution / scoring notes: Velocity is the derivative of position.
U4-S3-WU2-M01
Section 4.3 | Warm Up 2 | Mastery | DOK 2
For $f(x)=- 3 x^{2} - 3 x - 3$, find $f'(0)$.

Write the first mathematical step before carrying it out.

Answer: $-3$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate term by term and evaluate at $x=0$.
U4-S3-WU3-C01
Section 4.3 | Warm Up 3 | Current | DOK 2
Use the symbol table to explain why a numerical value for $r$ should normally be substituted after differentiating a relation between $r$ and $V$.
SymbolMeaning
$r$radius
$dr/dt$radius rate
$V$volume
$dV/dt$volume rate

Use the most efficient valid method available.

Answer: Substituting first can turn a changing quantity into a constant and erase its derivative factor.
Solution / scoring notes: Keep changing quantities symbolic until the time-derivative equation is established.
U4-S3-WU3-L01
Section 4.3 | Warm Up 3 | Last | DOK 3
A student argues that a positive acceleration always means a particle is speeding up. Give a counterexample using signs of velocity and acceleration, and state the correct criterion.

Use the most efficient valid method available.

Answer: For example, velocity negative and acceleration positive gives slowing down. A particle speeds up when velocity and acceleration have the same sign.
Solution / scoring notes: Acceleration sign alone is insufficient; compare it with velocity sign.
U4-S3-WU3-M01
Section 4.3 | Warm Up 3 | Mastery | DOK 1
Find the average rate of change of $g(x)=2 x - 2$ on $[-2,2]$.

Use the most efficient valid method available.

Answer: $2$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{g(b)-g(a)}{b-a}$.

Section 4.4 - Related Rates Problems

Section 4.4 | Warm Up 1 | Current | DOK 2
Water height in a cylinder of fixed radius $4$ m rises at $2$ m/min. Find $dV/dt$.

Use a direct method and keep the response concise.

Answer: $32 \pi$ m$^3$/min
Solution / scoring notes: Use $V=\pi r^2h$ with fixed radius and differentiate.
U4-S4-WU1-L01
Section 4.4 | Warm Up 1 | Last | DOK 2
Use the symbol table to explain why a numerical value for $r$ should normally be substituted after differentiating a relation between $r$ and $V$.
SymbolMeaning
$r$radius
$dr/dt$radius rate
$V$volume
$dV/dt$volume rate

Use a direct method and keep the response concise.

Answer: Substituting first can turn a changing quantity into a constant and erase its derivative factor.
Solution / scoring notes: Keep changing quantities symbolic until the time-derivative equation is established.
U4-S4-WU1-M01
Section 4.4 | Warm Up 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Differentiate $Q(x)=\frac{\sin{\left(x \right)} + 2}{x + 2}$.

Use a direct method and keep the response concise.

Answer: $Q'(x)=\frac{\left(x + 2\right) \cos{\left(x \right)} - \sin{\left(x \right)} - 2}{\left(x + 2\right)^{2}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the quotient rule and simplify.
U4-S4-WU2-C01
Section 4.4 | Warm Up 2 | Current | DOK 2
A rectangle has $L=6$ cm and $W=5$ cm at an instant. If $dL/dt=2$ cm/s and $dW/dt=1$ cm/s, find $dA/dt$.

Write the first mathematical step before carrying it out.

Answer: $16$ cm$^2$/s
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate $A=LW$ using the product rule and substitute the instant data.
U4-S4-WU2-L01
Section 4.4 | Warm Up 2 | Last | DOK 2
A rectangle has changing length $L$ and width $W$. Its area is $A=LW$. Write the time-differentiated relation needed for a related-rate setup.

Write the first mathematical step before carrying it out.

Answer: $dA/dt=L\,dW/dt+W\,dL/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate the product of two changing quantities with respect to time.
U4-S4-WU2-M01
Section 4.4 | Warm Up 2 | Mastery | DOK 2
For $f(x)=5 x^{4} + x + 2$, find $f'(-1)$.

Write the first mathematical step before carrying it out.

Answer: $-19$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate term by term and evaluate at $x=-1$.
U4-S4-WU3-C01
Section 4.4 | Warm Up 3 | Current | DOK 3
A circle expands with $dr/dt\gt0$. A student reports $dA/dt=-12\pi$ square units per second at a positive radius. Explain why the sign is inconsistent with the model, even before recomputing.

Use the most efficient valid method available.

Answer: Because $dA/dt=2\pi r\,dr/dt$ and both $r$ and $dr/dt$ are positive, area must be increasing.
Solution / scoring notes: Use the differentiated constraint to test sign reasonableness.
U4-S4-WU3-L01
Section 4.4 | Warm Up 3 | Last | DOK 3
A student immediately replaces $r$ by $5$ in $A=\pi r^2$ before differentiating a growing-circle problem. Explain what information can be lost and give the correct sequence.

Use the most efficient valid method available.

Answer: Keep $r$ symbolic, differentiate $dA/dt=2\pi r\,dr/dt$, then substitute the instant values.
Solution / scoring notes: Early substitution can incorrectly make a changing quantity appear constant.
U4-S4-WU3-M01
Section 4.4 | Warm Up 3 | Mastery | DOK 1
Find the average rate of change of $g(x)=4 x - 5$ on $[-3,-1]$.

Use the most efficient valid method available.

Answer: $4$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{g(b)-g(a)}{b-a}$.

Section 4.5 - Linearization and L'Hospital's Rule

Classify the limit form $0/0$ for purposes of deciding whether a direct application of L'Hospital's Rule is justified.

Use a direct method and keep the response concise.

Answer: indeterminate
Solution / scoring notes: Direct L'Hospital quotient forms are $0/0$ and $\infty/\infty$, provided the other conditions are satisfied.
U4-S5-WU1-L01

Differentiate $Q(x)=\frac{3 x^{2} + 3 x - 4}{x - 3}$.

Use a direct method and keep the response concise.

Answer: $Q'(x)=\frac{3 x^{2} - 18 x - 5}{x^{2} - 6 x + 9}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the quotient rule and simplify.
U4-S5-WU2-C01

Write the first mathematical step before carrying it out.

Answer: $3$
Solution / scoring notes: Direct substitution gives $0/0$. Differentiate numerator and denominator to get $3e^{3x}$, then evaluate at 0.
U4-S5-WU2-L01
Section 4.5 | Warm Up 2 | Last | DOK 3
For a sphere whose radius changes with time, compare the usefulness of $V=\frac43\pi r^3$ and $r=(3V/(4\pi))^{1/3}$ as starting constraints for finding $dV/dt$ from $dr/dt$. Choose one and defend the choice.

Write the first mathematical step before carrying it out.

Answer: The standard volume form is usually more efficient because differentiating gives $dV/dt=4\pi r^2\,dr/dt$ directly.
Solution / scoring notes: Both equivalent equations are valid on the physical domain, but one isolates the requested rate more directly.

Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate term by term and evaluate at $x=0$.

Use the most efficient valid method available.

Answer: Yes; $3$
Solution / scoring notes: The form is $\infty/\infty$. Differentiating numerator and denominator gives a ratio tending to $3$.

Section 4.1 - Meaning of Derivatives

U4-S1-ET-A-C01

At $t=3$, $f(3)=8$ where $f(t)$ represents the mass of salt in a solution. Interpret the value with units.

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: At $t=3$ minutes, the mass of salt in a solution is 8 grams.
Solution / scoring notes: A function value describes the original quantity, not a rate.
U4-S1-ET-A-C02
Section 4.1 | Exit Ticket A | Current | DOK 1
For a model of water depth in a tank, $f'(6)=-6$. Interpret this derivative in context and give units.

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: At $t=6$ seconds, the water depth in a tank is decreasing at 6 centimeters per seconds.
Solution / scoring notes: The first derivative is the instantaneous rate of change of the modeled quantity.

Answer: $2 \left(x^{2} + x + 1\right) e^{2 x}$
Solution / scoring notes: Coordinate product and chain rules.

Solution / scoring notes: The changing variables remain symbolic through differentiation.

A square has side length $s$ that changes with time. Write a constraint equation relating area $A$ and $s$, then write its time-differentiated form. Do not substitute $s=12$ yet.

Answer: $A=s^2$, $dA/dt=2s\,ds/dt$
Solution / scoring notes: The changing variables remain symbolic through differentiation.
U4-S4-ET-B-M01
Section 4.4 | Exit Ticket B | Mastery | DOK 2
Evaluate $\lim_{x\to -3}\frac{x^{4} - 81}{x + 3}$.

Use precise notation in the final response.

Answer: $-108$
Solution / scoring notes: Factor and cancel the common factor; the remaining expression approaches $-108$.
U4-S4-ET-C-C01
Section 4.4 | Exit Ticket C | Current | DOK 3
For a sphere whose radius changes with time, compare the usefulness of $V=\frac43\pi r^3$ and $r=(3V/(4\pi))^{1/3}$ as starting constraints for finding $dV/dt$ from $dr/dt$. Choose one and defend the choice.

Check the sign, notation, or units before finalizing the response.

Answer: The standard volume form is usually more efficient because differentiating gives $dV/dt=4\pi r^2\,dr/dt$ directly.
Solution / scoring notes: Both equivalent equations are valid on the physical domain, but one isolates the requested rate more directly.
U4-S4-ET-C-C02

Solve a related-rate problem in words: identify the constraint, show the differentiated equation before substitution, and explain how the sign of the final rate determines direction.

Check the sign, notation, or units before finalizing the response.

Answer: A complete response must show a valid context-specific constraint, its time derivative, substitution only afterward, and a sign/units interpretation.
Solution / scoring notes: This item assesses the complete routine rather than a single formula.
U4-S4-ET-C-L01
Section 4.4 | Exit Ticket C | Last | DOK 1
A circular oil slick has changing radius $r$ centimeters. Its radius increases at $3$ cm/min. At the instant $r=10$ cm, classify $r$, $dr/dt$, and $10$ as a quantity, a rate, or the instant-specific value.

Check the sign, notation, or units before finalizing the response.

Answer: $r$ is the changing quantity; $dr/dt=3$ cm/min is a rate; $r=10$ cm is the value at the instant.
Solution / scoring notes: Separate variable, derivative, and specific value before differentiating.

Answer: $4$

Section 4.5 - Linearization and L'Hospital's Rule

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: $\frac{18}{5}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $L(x)=f(a)+f'(a)(x-a)$.

Answer: $\frac{899}{300}$
Solution / scoring notes: Build $L(x)=f(a)+f'(a)(x-a)$ at the convenient square $9$.
U4-S5-ET-A-L01
Section 4.5 | Exit Ticket A | Last | DOK 2
A $13$-ft ladder rests against a wall. The bottom moves away at $1$ ft/s. When the bottom is $5$ ft from the wall, how fast is the top moving?

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: $- \frac{5}{12}$ ft/s
Solution / scoring notes: Use $x^2+y^2=169$; differentiate to $x\,dx/dt+y\,dy/dt=0$. The negative result means downward.

QuantityValue
$f(3)$$5$
$f'(3)$$-2$
$g(3)$$-7$
$g'(3)$$2$

Answer: $24$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the product rule using the four table values.
U4-S5-ET-B-C01
Section 4.5 | Exit Ticket B | Current | DOK 3
Compare linearization and L'Hospital's Rule as methods: state what question each method answers and one condition or limitation that must be checked before use.

Use precise notation in the final response.

Answer: Linearization estimates a function locally from a tangent model; L'Hospital evaluates certain indeterminate quotient limits. Locality matters for linearization, and the quotient must have an appropriate indeterminate form plus rule conditions for L'Hospital.
Solution / scoring notes: The methods serve different purposes and are not interchangeable.

Answer: $\frac{39}{10}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $L(x)=f(a)+f'(a)(x-a)$.
