U4-S1-PERF01
Section 4.1 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 3

Performance / Future Task

Audit three derivative statements for a population model measured in organisms and hours: $P(4)=120$, $P'(4)=-8$, and $P''(4)=3$. Write a context-specific interpretation with units for each, identify one statement that would be misleading if units were omitted, and explain why.
Expected evidence: Three interpretations; correct units; distinction among derivative orders; critique.
Scoring criteria: Three interpretations; correct units; distinction among derivative orders; critique.
Estimated student time: 10-12 minutes
Answer: Interpretations must distinguish amount, rate, and rate-of-rate with organisms, organisms/hour, and organisms/hour squared.
Solution / scoring notes: Three correct interpretations and a justified units critique.

Section 4.3 - Related Rates Intro

U4-S3-PERF01
Section 4.3 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 3

Performance / Future Task

Design a reusable setup protocol for related-rates problems and test it on two contexts: a 13-ft ladder with changing horizontal and vertical distances, and a growing circle with changing radius and area. For each, label quantities, rates, constants, constraint, instant-of-interest data, and the derivative equation. Do not complete numerical solves. Revise the protocol if either test exposes a missing step.
Expected evidence: Variables/rates/constants; constraints; time derivatives; substitution timing; transferable protocol.
Scoring criteria: Variables/rates/constants; constraints; time derivatives; substitution timing; transferable protocol.
Estimated student time: 12-15 minutes
Answer: A robust protocol separates quantities from rates, writes the constraint before differentiating, differentiates with respect to time, and postpones instant substitution.
Solution / scoring notes: Two complete setups and a revised protocol if needed.

Section 4.4 - Related Rates Problems

U4-S4-PERF01
Section 4.4 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 4

Performance / Future Task

A spherical weather balloon has radius 4 m and is expanding at 0.6 m/min. Produce a complete related-rates solution for the volume rate, then create a second solution pathway from an algebraically equivalent radius-in-terms-of-volume equation. Compare the two pathways, verify agreement, and defend which is clearer for this requested rate.
Expected evidence: Two valid setups; correct rate; units/direction; agreement check; efficiency defense.
Scoring criteria: Two valid setups; correct rate; units/direction; agreement check; efficiency defense.
Estimated student time: 15-18 minutes
Answer: $4\pi(4)^2(0.6)=38.4\pi$ m$^3$/min
Solution / scoring notes: Both valid pathways must agree; the standard volume constraint is generally more direct for dV/dt.

Section 4.5 - Linearization and L'Hospital's Rule

Performance / Future Task

Use the displayed function $f$ and tangent line $L$ centered at $x=-1$ to conduct a local-linearity study. Compare function and tangent estimates at three distances from the center, describe how approximation error changes, and write a condition-aware statement of what local linearity does and does not promise. Then contrast that use of a tangent line with the purpose of L'Hospital's Rule for an indeterminate quotient limit.
Expected evidence: Three comparisons; error reasoning; locality condition; correct L'Hospital contrast.
Scoring criteria: Three comparisons; error reasoning; locality condition; correct L'Hospital contrast.
Estimated student time: 15-20 minutes
Answer: A successful response documents increasing-distance comparisons, an evidence-based locality claim, and a correct contrast with L'Hospital as a limit method.
Solution / scoring notes: Data comparisons; error trend; revised locality statement; method-purpose contrast.

DOK 2 - Motion Sign Analysis

U4-FA-D2-02-V1
Section 4.2 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Motion Sign Analysis

At an instant, $v=8$ m/s and $a=-4$ m/s$^2$. Determine direction and whether the particle is speeding up or slowing down; justify by signs.
Answer: Positive direction; slowing down.
Solution / scoring notes: Velocity gives direction; same signs mean speeding up, opposite signs slowing down.

DOK 3 - Context Claim Critique

U4-FA-D3-01-V1
Section 4.1 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

Context Claim Critique

A student writes: “Because $f'(2)=2$, the volume of fuel in a reservoir equals 2 liters at time 2.” Critique the claim and rewrite it correctly.
Answer: The derivative is a rate, not the original quantity; it means the volume of fuel in a reservoir is increasing at 2 liters per minutes at that instant.
Solution / scoring notes: Repair meaning and units.

DOK 3 - Related Rates Model Choice

U4-FA-D3-03-V1
Section 4.4 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

Related Rates Model Choice

A growing spherical balloon is modeled either by $V=\frac43\pi r^3$ or by $r=(3V/(4\pi))^{1/3}$. If $dr/dt$ is given and $dV/dt$ is requested, choose the more efficient starting equation and defend the choice without claiming the other equation is invalid.
Answer: Use the standard volume equation; its time derivative isolates $dV/dt$ directly in terms of $r$ and $dr/dt$.
Solution / scoring notes: Compare valid models by efficiency and target rate.

DOK 3 - Method Conditions Analysis

U4-FA-D3-04-V1
Section 4.5 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

Method Conditions Analysis

For each limit, decide whether direct substitution already resolves it or whether L'Hospital's Rule is justified. Defend each decision using the resulting form: $\lim_{x\to2}\frac{x^2+1}{x+3}$, $\lim_{x\to0}\frac{e^x-1}{x}$, $\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{2x^2+1}{x^2+4}$.
Answer: First resolves directly; second gives $0/0$ and permits L'Hospital; third gives $\infty/\infty$ and permits L'Hospital under the standard differentiability conditions.
Solution / scoring notes: Method choice must follow the limit form, not merely the presence of a quotient.

Section 4.5 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

Method Conditions Analysis

For each limit, decide whether direct substitution already resolves it or whether L'Hospital's Rule is justified. Defend each decision using the resulting form: $\lim_{x\to2}\frac{x^2+1}{x+3}$, $\lim_{x\to0}\frac{e^x-1}{x}$, $\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{2x^2+1}{x^2+4}$.

Use a complete sentence when the response is an interpretation. Focus the response on the method conditions requested here.

Answer: First resolves directly; second gives $0/0$ and permits L'Hospital; third gives $\infty/\infty$ and permits L'Hospital under the standard differentiability conditions.
Solution / scoring notes: Method choice must follow the limit form, not merely the presence of a quotient.

11. Summative Assessment Source Content

Summative Version 1

U4-SUM-V1-Q01
Section 4.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 1

Unit 4 Summative - Version 1

If $f'(3)=5$ for a model of electrical charge stored in a device, which interpretation is correct?
  1. The electrical charge stored in a device is increasing at 5 coulombs per seconds.
  2. The electrical charge stored in a device equals 5 coulombs.
  3. The electrical charge stored in a device is changing at 5 coulombs per seconds squared.
  4. The time is changing at 5 seconds per coulombs.
Answer: A. The electrical charge stored in a device is increasing at 5 coulombs per seconds.
Solution / scoring notes: A first derivative is a contextual rate with output units per input unit.
U4-SUM-V1-Q02
Section 4.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 4 Summative - Version 1

For a model of electrical charge stored in a device, interpret $f''(5)=2$ with units.
Answer: At time 5 seconds, the rate of change of the electrical charge stored in a device is increasing at 2 coulombs per seconds squared.
Solution / scoring notes: Interpret the second derivative as rate-of-rate.
U4-SUM-V1-Q03

In one time-based context, what do $f(t)$, $f'(t)$, and $f''(t)$ represent, respectively?

  4. quantity, rate of rate, rate

U4-SUM-V1-Q04
Section 4.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 3

Unit 4 Summative - Version 1

A student says, “The derivative value is $-4$, so the modeled quantity is negative.” Critique the inference and describe what additional context is needed to interpret the derivative correctly.
Answer: A negative derivative means the quantity is decreasing at that instant, not necessarily that the quantity itself is negative. The quantity meaning, units, and independent-variable units are needed.
Solution / scoring notes: Separate sign of the rate from sign of the original quantity.

Solution / scoring notes: Method choice is justified by preserving time dependence.

U4-SUM-V1-Q19
Section 4.5 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 1

Unit 4 Summative - Version 1

A quotient limit has direct-substitution form $\infty/\infty$. Which statement best describes a direct L'Hospital decision?

  2. may apply after checking the remaining conditions
  3. may apply only if the numerator is polynomial
  4. must always apply

Solution / scoring notes: The direct quotient rule is reserved for 0/0 or infinity/infinity, with conditions.
U4-SUM-V1-Q20
Section 4.5 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 3

Unit 4 Summative - Version 1

Compare these two decisions: (A) use a tangent-line approximation for a nearby function value, and (B) use L'Hospital's Rule for a quotient limit. State the condition or purpose that justifies each, and explain why merely seeing a derivative or a quotient is not enough.
Answer: A uses local linearity near a center where function value and slope define a tangent model; B requires an appropriate indeterminate quotient form and rule conditions. The methods answer different questions.
Solution / scoring notes: Strategic method analysis must include conditions and purpose.
